# Spike: Enola call coverage

## Verdict

Enola v0.4.27 is not good enough to budget search on Melian. It matches **825/5,723 call pairs (14.4%)** and **207/407 import edges (50.9%)**. Production files alone reach 825/1,643 calls (50.2%). Five files with calls reach 100%; 21 files have no repository call pairs and are n/a. Search stays unrestricted over every file.

The exit criterion is met: per-file coverage is defined and measured against the same compiler project as static.tsc, with every unmatched pair and import named below. Matching a graph edge does not prove behaviour. The layering constraint is proved below. Caller plumbing remains a separate deliverable.

Measured commit: `ad303b56fac7e40b13a1a7e51140fa05a9a4b570`, origin/main at the branch point. Tree: `06dd48e8c19ca89cc03589b772c7ee39a5e6b1b9`. Ground truth includes 122 files, including 47 test files. Root tsconfig includes source and tests directly; it has no project references. Enola scans 962 files, identifies 67 test files, and skips 68. Its extra files are outside the compiler denominator. External calls (13,019) and unresolved calls (67) are counted separately.

## Measurement

| Unit | Matched | Compiler units | Coverage | Named gaps |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Call pairs, all files | 825 | 5,723 | 14.4% | 4,898 |
| Call pairs, production | 825 | 1,643 | 50.2% | 818 |
| Import edges, all files | 207 | 407 | 50.9% | 200 |

The [per-file table](#per-file-table) and the gap list retain every denominator and missing unit. The exit criterion measures coverage; it does not require a passing coverage threshold.

## Definition and method

Each import or re-export declaration and literal dynamic import whose specifier resolves to a repository source file is one import edge. Each distinct enclosing-function/callee pair is one call unit for calls, new expressions, and tagged templates, following aliases. Identify declarations by file, line, column, and qualified name. A named function value uses its binding declaration, including a multiline initializer. Anonymous functions retain their own identity; getters and setters are enclosing methods. Top-level calls use a module sentinel. Exclude declaration files, node_modules, and libraries from the denominator. Zero units means n/a, never 100%.

TypeScript 7.0.2's unstable synchronous API supplies the program, checker, aliases, and signatures. No second compiler dependency was added. [CompilerGraph](../../packages/pipeline/src/compiler-graph.ts) closes its snapshot and process and has a dedicated package entry, so ordinary pipeline users do not load the AST modules. [The spike script](../../packages/evals/src/enola-spike.ts) runs Enola through the execution environment on a disposable copy of the trusted commit. Workspace links point into that copy, so tsc cannot resolve to newer working-tree code. Fixtures prove a package alias, re-export, repeated-call deduplication, test caller, unresolved dynamic call, multiline binding, getter, and file-name casing by hand.

Facts prove a call only through an explicit resolved target_id. Impact proves a call only through a calls or instantiates edge and the caller's declaration location. has_method is not a call. Facts carry 824 pairs; impact carries 807; their union carries 825. This upstream extractor resolves many cross-package fact edges the earlier fork measurement left dangling. Combining both contracts adds one pair through impact without discarding facts-only matches. Melian recreates no Enola algorithm.

Import matches require an explicit target file or an upstream imports edge identifying the importing declaration. Impact added no import matches. A directory module cannot prove which file imported a symbol. Names shared across files in a directory are ambiguous; those pairs cannot prove a match. This prevents collisions from faking coverage. file_ref nodes represent top-level calls, not calling functions.

The run made 786 unique impact queries, depth 1 and at most 500 nodes. Nine supplied no exact answer. Four exited 2: piCredentialStore, marker, toolLog, and guardrail. Five exited 0 but selected another full name: configLookup -> ConfigLookup, declaredBudget -> DeclaredBudget, staticToolSource -> StaticToolSource, fakeState -> FakeState, and runIdentity -> RunIdentity. All use the directory prefixes in their fact names. Lens queries use the smaller cap of 50 nodes and also require the returned target to agree. A successful empty report has edges:null; that is accepted only with zero dependents and an empty depth map. An exit-2 document never means no callers.

## Gaps by primary cause

Each missing unit gets one primary cause. Test exclusion takes precedence, then ambiguous names, then declaration and edge failures. Thus a test call through a method still counts under test exclusion. A residual resolved-edge failure states what the contract lacks; it does not invent an extractor explanation.

| Unit | Cause | Lost units |
|---|---|---:|
| call | anonymous callback or function value | 361 |
| call | class construction | 125 |
| call | cross-directory or barrel resolution | 90 |
| call | directory-scoped name collision | 43 |
| call | interface or union method declaration | 30 |
| call | nested named function value absent | 80 |
| call | private method absent | 10 |
| call | resolved declaration edge absent | 67 |
| call | test file excluded | 4080 |
| call | this method dispatch | 12 |
| import | test file excluded | 154 |
| import | type-only import | 6 |
| import | workspace package alias unresolved in facts | 40 |

Tagged templates, JSON imports, and literal dynamic imports lose zero units in this tree. Re-export imports match where facts retain the target file. require is outside the import-declaration definition; its library calls are external. Passing a callback as an argument is not itself a call unit. Unresolved invocations remain in the separate unresolved count. Every pair or edge lost to tests, callbacks, nested values, private methods, interface declarations, this dispatch, construction, barrels, and collisions is listed below.

A later experiment could propose graphCoverage.searchThreshold = 1.0 for both imports and calls, requiring unambiguous identities. This is not a supported or applied setting. Before budgeting search, Enola needs test bodies, anonymous and nested function identities, private methods, receiver dispatch, construction edges, and precise file-level imports. The residual resolved-edge gaps need upstream reproductions. Dynamic behaviour still needs execution.

## Cost and cache

| Run | Wall clock | Peak resident memory |
|---|---:|---:|
| generate-cold | 5.096 s | 89.9 MB |
| generate-warm | 2.098 s | 77.4 MB |
| impact-cold | 2.354 s | 71.4 MB |
| impact-warm | 1.326 s | 39.5 MB |

Cold means no snapshot or extractor cache, not a flushed operating-system page cache. Warm generation recomputes with extractor reuse; warm impact restores the snapshot. Wall clock includes the execution wrapper. Peak memory comes from the native subprocess through /usr/bin/time. These are the final run's values on a shared machine under load. Earlier complete runs measured cold generation at 1.022 and 14.143 seconds, and warm generation at 0.406 and 6.694 seconds. The earlier research measured 0.44 seconds and 78 MB on a different 325-file tree; neither its tree nor machine load is the same.

The entry holds 2,948,081 bytes. A separately measured fresh publication took 0.006 seconds after generation; a validated hit took 0.059 seconds. The benchmark removes only its own scratch entry before measuring a miss. A complete static probe took 4.08 seconds with a miss and 2.73 seconds with hits, including worktrees, baseline pinning, and SARIF. This size does not justify a prune command.

Two generations produced identical fact bytes and the same snapshot ID. Receipts differ in timestamps, duration, and path. Cache key: `962f4649c93d4b168a99b8151916c51049b5ff2d16b8b3046b930e4112a09626`. Coverage content ID: `d239e5deea039ad35e46b0f5dcb227a074e53f471957588673843d9c8e2d1707`. Cache reads check artifact hashes, receipt format, and key parts. Corruption is a miss.

## What was proved and what was not

The official archive was verified before extraction. Its executable is enola-0.4.27-darwin-arm64; it also holds LICENSE and NOTICE. All four platform hashes agree with GitHub's API and the release checksum files. The young release has an explicit quarantine exception because this step needs impact. It ages out at 2026-10-07 16:37:02 UTC. There is no fork or updater.

The static probe proves that two temporary worktrees agree on repository identity, generate graphs, pin base, and compare head. Providers and history are disabled, with temporary HOME and output. Tests use local fake archives and scripts, never real downloads, providers, or GitHub calls. Bounds, digest mismatch, unsafe tar entries, young releases, wrong hosts, swapped binaries, and exit-2 queries have refusal tests.

This spike does not prove runtime dispatch, execution paths, test coverage, or semantic correctness. Test coverage stays unavailable until container isolation. Search remains unrestricted. The sandbox denies new worktrees in the original common directory, so real probes use exempt temporary repositories fetched locally from the named commit, with no remote added. That preserves the exact tree. The requested external scratch directory is denied; logs and measurements use ignored tmp/step11-scratch in this worktree.

The first full gate ran beside the measurement and hit ten five-second timeouts. The SQLite dismissal and the golden cases passed in isolated retries. The compiler's dedicated entry avoids loading its AST modules in unrelated pipeline and evals users. No global timeout was raised.

## Layering proof

The directory-only core-to-pipeline rule failed to catch a scratch `@melian-agent/pipeline` import. Enola emitted a skipped-target advisory in insights and no SARIF findings; check exited 0. Alias globs also failed because grounding requires a measured file. The final policy uses dependency facts as the source, named package targets for workspace imports, and a directory target for resolved paths.

`constraints init` found no recipe whose directories bound. `constraints lint` accepted the committed file and resolved 41 core-import members and 514 pipeline members. On the ad303b5 tree with the policy, clean check exited 0 with no results. A tracked scratch file imported `@melian-agent/pipeline`, `@melian-agent/pipeline/testing` and `../../pipeline/src/static.ts`. Check exited 1 and reported three errors at lines 1, 2 and 3 under the package and path rules. Providers and history were disabled; HOME was temporary.

The harness-free test remains broader and deterministic. It forbids Pi and every other Melian package, including import forms the extractor may miss. Neither guard makes the other redundant.
